        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.
- Subagent design critique to fold into final document/review: guide users by making the desired structure the shortest path. Common intents include ready-template overrides, multi-stage graph execution, graph -> Python -> graph transforms, opaque subgraph reuse, and manual ready-template migration. Public concepts should be Template, typed Handle[T], RunResult/Artifact, Pipeline/ordinary async Python. First-class: ready Python builders, blocks, patches, typed requirements, multi-run orchestration, artifact passing. Escape hatches: raw add_node/connect, raw refs, opaque subgraphs, compile('api'/'graphbuilder'), raw API import/export, raw HiddenSwitch config. Add guardrail/lint ideas: untyped_raw_ref, stale_metadata, unbound_input_unused, artifact_vs_node_handle, ready_not_runtime_green, widget_index_escape, unknown_custom_node_commit, http_queue_only. Emphasize arbitrary Python belongs at orchestration boundaries; runtime Python inside a graph must be a custom/external node. Every high-level object should reveal lower layers for debugging: template.workflow, handle.node_id/slot, result.outputs, wf.compile('api'), wf.explain(), wf.doctor().

      "description": "Append three custom-nodes sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

      "id": "T5",
      "description": "Append three relationship/limits sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder` \u2014 keep `compile(\"graphbuilder\")` *optional* per `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md` (SD-001). Why GraphBuilder is **not** the authoring surface: ties authoring to an installed runtime, no typed handles beyond `.out(int)`, no provenance/metadata, no diff-friendly Python output. Borrow GraphBuilder's `id=` argument convention; use it as a parity-test target. (2) `## Relationship to ComfyScript` \u2014 `learn`, not `adopt` (SD-002). Future one-way import adapter only (ComfyScript script \u2192 `VibeWorkflow`). Reasoning: requires live `/object_info`, emits ComfyScript-flavored APIs, breaks on `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes (cite `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`). (3) `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` \u2014 HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Workarounds: (i) author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run via `run_embedded_sync`; (ii) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner that compiles a minimal graph terminating at a sink inferred from the handle's output type. **Prerequisite: `wf.run_until` is gated on the typed-`Handle` rollout AND on a populated schema snapshot (lands in P4). Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves. Do not promise inferred taps before typed handles land.** Cite `docs/runtime_surface.md` and `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`.",

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy

        Output path (write all sections here):
        docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md

        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.
- Subagent design critique to fold into final document/review: guide users by making the desired structure the shortest path. Common intents include ready-template overrides, multi-stage graph execution, graph -> Python -> graph transforms, opaque subgraph reuse, and manual ready-template migration. Public concepts should be Template, typed Handle[T], RunResult/Artifact, Pipeline/ordinary async Python. First-class: ready Python builders, blocks, patches, typed requirements, multi-run orchestration, artifact passing. Escape hatches: raw add_node/connect, raw refs, opaque subgraphs, compile('api'/'graphbuilder'), raw API import/export, raw HiddenSwitch config. Add guardrail/lint ideas: untyped_raw_ref, stale_metadata, unbound_input_unused, artifact_vs_node_handle, ready_not_runtime_green, widget_index_escape, unknown_custom_node_commit, http_queue_only. Emphasize arbitrary Python belongs at orchestration boundaries; runtime Python inside a graph must be a custom/external node. Every high-level object should reveal lower layers for debugging: template.workflow, handle.node_id/slot, result.outputs, wf.compile('api'), wf.explain(), wf.doctor().

      "description": "Append three sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md` covering the typed-handle / orchestration / runtime-Python triad. (1) `## Typed Handles (and Backward Compatibility)` \u2014 define `Handle` as carrying `(node_id, output_slot, output_type | None, name | None)`. State explicitly that `str(handle)` returns the existing `f\"{node_id}.{slot}\"` form so all current consumers keep working unchanged; cite `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47` and `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:12` (which wraps arbitrary values today, making the typed `Handle` additive). New code can opt into `.out(\"image\")` and `.type` accessors; recipes adopt incrementally \u2014 no flag day. (2) `## Multi-Stage Orchestration (Comfy \u21c4 Python)` \u2014 primary mixing model is `Graph A \u2192 typed outputs/files/metadata \u2192 ordinary Python transform/decision \u2192 Graph B`. State the rules: arbitrary Python runs *between* graph executions; arbitrary Python runs *inside* a graph only by compiling to an `ExternalPythonNode`. Define `VibeFlow` as holding an ordered list of `Stage` entries (each is either a `VibeWorkflow` to execute or a `Callable[..., StageResult]`); the executor passes typed media handles between stages. (3) `## ExternalPythonNode (Distinct from @block)` \u2014 explicit FLAG-005-resolving subsection. State that the authoring-time `@block` protocol (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) **mutates a `VibeWorkflow` and returns `Handles`** and **never executes during Comfy runtime**; that `ExternalPythonNode` is a **runtime** primitive emitting a real Comfy custom node (or external-process node) registered through `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` with explicit serialization boundaries on its inputs and outputs (typed file/tensor handles only \u2014 no Python objects across the boundary); that the two are deliberately named differently to prevent collision; that `@block` is authoring-only sugar and `ExternalPythonNode` is the only sanctioned way to run Python inside an active graph. Include this comparison table verbatim:\n\n| Surface | When it runs | Where it lives | Returns |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `@block` (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) | Authoring time | Python builder code | `Handles` |\n| `ExternalPythonNode` | Comfy runtime | Compiled to a Comfy custom node + `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` | Comfy-typed outputs (IMAGE/LATENT/STRING/\u2026) |",

(1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md\n+# Python Composition DSL Plan\n+\n+## Settled Decisions\n+\n+- **SD-001** - Keep `VibeWorkflow` as the canonical editable IR. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: the current package already normalizes, edits, validates, and compiles through `VibeWorkflow`; the composition layer should make that model easier to author, not replace it.\n+- **SD-002** - Treat HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder as an optional backend, not the authoring surface. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: `compile(\"graphbuilder\")` exists for parity and runtime alignment, while direct `compile(\"api\")` remains the transparent handoff path.\n+- **SD-003** - Treat ComfyScript as reference material and a possible import adapter, not the primary API. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: the plan needs VibeComfy-native Python that can be edited, linted, and traced without depending on live ComfyScript generation.\n+- **SD-004** - Make multi-stage Python orchestration the default model for mixing Comfy and ordinary Python. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is clearer and safer than injecting arbitrary Python into an active Comfy graph.\n+- **SD-005** - Keep arbitrary Python outside active Comfy graph execution unless it is wrapped as a runtime node. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: Comfy graph execution has serialization and runtime boundaries that ordinary Python objects do not satisfy.\n+- **SD-006** - Pin custom nodepacks by repository and immutable `git_commit_sha` when specified, with lockfile capture when defaulting to newest. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: custom node behavior is part of workflow reproducibility.\n+- **SD-007** - Provide escape hatches for raw workflow, raw JSON, raw node, and raw runtime configuration paths. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: the layer must improve common authoring without blocking workflows whose nodes or schemas are not typed yet.\n+- **SD-008** - Migrate ready templates manually and iteratively, using the Flux 4B native builder as the model. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: the repository currently contains many runtime-green API dictionaries; a big-bang migration would add risk without improving execution.\n+- **SD-009** - Add structured provenance for composition, source, and multi-stage flow execution. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: authors need to explain where a graph came from, what changed, and what artifacts moved between stages.\n+- **SD-010** - Make typed handles first-class and backward compatible with existing string references. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: typed handles unlock debugging and validation while preserving existing `node.slot` wiring conventions.\n+- **SD-011** - Gate direct debug execution helpers on typed handles and schema-backed output metadata. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: VibeComfy cannot infer safe preview/save taps until handles know their output types.\n+- **SD-012** - Keep this delivery to documentation only. _load_bearing: false_\n+  Rationale: implementation details can follow after the architecture and constraints are explicit.\n+\n+## Goals\n+\n+- Provide a concise pure-Python authoring layer for users who want to tweak, refine, update, and combine ComfyUI workflows without hand-editing API dictionaries.\n+- Keep execution through HiddenSwitch and ComfyUI: Python composition builds `VibeWorkflow`, `VibeWorkflow` compiles to Comfy API JSON, and HiddenSwitch executes that graph.\n+- Make multi-stage orchestration first-class so ordinary Python can run before, after, and between Comfy graph executions using typed media/file handles.\n+- Add typed handles, artifacts, and provenance hooks that make debugging and workflow explanation easier than raw `node.slot` strings.\n+- Work with the current VibeComfy package without forking HiddenSwitch, ComfyUI, or the existing `VibeWorkflow` storage model.\n+\n+## Non-Goals / Constraints\n+\n+- This is not a new runtime. HiddenSwitch remains the execution boundary, either through embedded `Comfy()` or server-backed queueing.\n+- This is not a parallel workflow IR. `VibeWorkflow` remains canonical, matching the current class at `vibecomfy/workflow.py:74`.\n+- Authoring must not require a live `/object_info` request. A checked-in or cached schema snapshot is enough for typed affordances; raw/opaque nodes remain possible when schema data is missing.\n+- No direct single-node execution is promised as a core runtime feature. HiddenSwitch accepts whole-graph API dictionaries via `/prompt` and embedded `Comfy.queue_prompt_api`, so VibeComfy should compile small graphs when it needs focused debug runs.\n+- No arbitrary Python is injected inside an active Comfy graph. Runtime Python inside a graph must cross an explicit node boundary, such as `ExternalPythonNode`, with serialized inputs and outputs.\n+\n+## Architecture\n+\n+```text\n+Python composition layer (new)\n+  - Template\n+  - Pipeline\n+  - Blocks\n+  - Patches\n+  - typed Handle[T]\n+  - RunResult / Artifact\n+          |\n+          v\n+VibeWorkflow (canonical IR)\n+  - nodes, edges, inputs, outputs, metadata\n+  - class defined at vibecomfy/workflow.py:74\n+          |\n+          +--> compile(\"api\") at vibecomfy/workflow.py:170\n+          |       |\n+          |       v\n+          |   HiddenSwitch / ComfyUI\n+          |     - embedded Comfy()\n+          |     - server /prompt\n+          |\n+          +--> compile(\"graphbuilder\") parity branch\n+          |       - backend implementation at vibecomfy/workflow.py:184\n+          |\n+          +--> ComfyScript import-only adapter\n+                  - future one-way import into VibeWorkflow\n+\n+Multi-stage flow:\n+\n+Stage(Python setup)\n+  -> Graph A (VibeWorkflow -> HiddenSwitch)\n+  -> Stage(Python transform / decision)\n+  -> Graph B (VibeWorkflow -> HiddenSwitch)\n+  -> Stage(Python finalize)\n+```\n+\n+The desired path should be the shortest path: author a `Template` or `Pipeline`, call blocks and patches, receive typed `Handle[T]` values, run one or more graphs, and inspect `RunResult` / `Artifact` objects. Lower layers remain visible for debugging and escape hatches.\n+\n+## Layer Definitions\n+\n+- **Nodes** - `wf.node(class_type, **kwargs)` is the typed authoring wrapper over `VibeWorkflow.add_node(...)`. It returns an object with `.out(name_or_index)` for a typed `Handle[T]`, while still compiling into the same node/edge model that `VibeWorkflow` owns.\n+- **Blocks** - `@block` functions are authoring-time helpers that mutate a `VibeWorkflow` and return handles. They are the local shape for reusable graph fragments, loaders, samplers, decoders, save nodes, and opaque subgraphs.\n+- **Recipes / Pipelines** - `Pipeline` is the public concept for higher-level ordinary Python that combines blocks, patches, templates, and multiple graph runs. A pipeline may be synchronous or async, but it should remain normal Python rather than a hidden graph runtime.\n+- **Patches** - patch objects decorate or transform an existing `VibeWorkflow` without changing the public authoring model. They remain the right shape for policies such as seed changes, model swaps, save-prefix edits, and topology-preserving runtime tweaks.\n+- **Templates** - `Template` is the published, reusable recipe shape. A template exposes its underlying `VibeWorkflow`, default requirements, metadata, and documented override points.\n+- **Stages** - a stage is one step in a multi-stage flow. It may be ordinary Python, a `VibeWorkflow` graph execution, or final artifact collection. Stage boundaries are where ordinary Python and Comfy execution exchange typed handles, files, and metadata.\n+- **Typed Handles** - `Handle[T]` names a node output or artifact with `node_id`, output slot, optional name, and eventual schema type. It is additive over today’s string references, not a flag-day replacement.\n+- **Run Results / Artifacts** - `RunResult` records execution metadata and maps named graph outputs to `Artifact` values such as images, audio, video, JSON metadata, or files. Artifacts are the Python-side values passed between stages.\n+- **ExternalPythonNode** - this is the runtime boundary for Python that must execute inside a Comfy graph. It is not the same concept as an authoring-time block.\n+- **Escape Hatches** - raw refs, raw node construction, opaque subgraphs, `compile(\"api\")`, `compile(\"graphbuilder\")`, raw API import/export, and raw HiddenSwitch configuration remain available when typed helpers are incomplete.\n*** Update File: docs/authoring.md\n@@\n # Authoring\n \n+> See [docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md](python_composition_dsl_plan.md) for the broader composition-layer architecture this doc fits into.\n+\n `VibeWorkflow` is the only editable IR. Blocks and patches mutate that object; API JSON is an escape hatch produced by `wf.compile(\"api\")`, not an authoring surface.\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.
- Subagent design critique to fold into final document/review: guide users by making the desired structure the shortest path. Common intents include ready-template overrides, multi-stage graph execution, graph -> Python -> graph transforms, opaque subgraph reuse, and manual ready-template migration. Public concepts should be Template, typed Handle[T], RunResult/Artifact, Pipeline/ordinary async Python. First-class: ready Python builders, blocks, patches, typed requirements, multi-run orchestration, artifact passing. Escape hatches: raw add_node/connect, raw refs, opaque subgraphs, compile('api'/'graphbuilder'), raw API import/export, raw HiddenSwitch config. Add guardrail/lint ideas: untyped_raw_ref, stale_metadata, unbound_input_unused, artifact_vs_node_handle, ready_not_runtime_green, widget_index_escape, unknown_custom_node_commit, http_queue_only. Emphasize arbitrary Python belongs at orchestration boundaries; runtime Python inside a graph must be a custom/external node. Every high-level object should reveal lower layers for debugging: template.workflow, handle.node_id/slot, result.outputs, wf.compile('api'), wf.explain(), wf.doctor().

      "description": "Append three custom-nodes sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

      "id": "T5",
      "description": "Append three relationship/limits sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder` \u2014 keep `compile(\"graphbuilder\")` *optional* per `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md` (SD-001). Why GraphBuilder is **not** the authoring surface: ties authoring to an installed runtime, no typed handles beyond `.out(int)`, no provenance/metadata, no diff-friendly Python output. Borrow GraphBuilder's `id=` argument convention; use it as a parity-test target. (2) `## Relationship to ComfyScript` \u2014 `learn`, not `adopt` (SD-002). Future one-way import adapter only (ComfyScript script \u2192 `VibeWorkflow`). Reasoning: requires live `/object_info`, emits ComfyScript-flavored APIs, breaks on `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes (cite `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`). (3) `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` \u2014 HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Workarounds: (i) author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run via `run_embedded_sync`; (ii) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner that compiles a minimal graph terminating at a sink inferred from the handle's output type. **Prerequisite: `wf.run_until` is gated on the typed-`Handle` rollout AND on a populated schema snapshot (lands in P4). Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves. Do not promise inferred taps before typed handles land.** Cite `docs/runtime_surface.md` and `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`.",

The required fields are the graph output identity; the optional fields are authoring and validation affordances that improve over time as schema snapshots get better.\n+\n+Backward compatibility is explicit: `str(handle)` returns the existing `f\"{node_id}.{slot}\"` form. Current consumers that expect string refs keep working unchanged, including the opaque subgraph convention that returns `f\"{node.id}.{slot}\"` today in `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47`.\n+\n+This is additive because `Handles` already wraps arbitrary values at `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:12`. A block can keep returning strings, or it can return typed handles where the recipe benefits from named outputs, `.type`, `.node_id`, and `.slot`.\n+\n+New code should prefer:\n+\n+```python\n+image = wf.node(\"VAEDecode\", samples=samples, vae=vae).out(\"image\")\n+wf.node(\"SaveImage\", images=image, filename_prefix=\"example\")\n+```\n+\n+Recipes migrate incrementally. There is no flag day: old `\"12.0\"` refs, `Handles(image=\"12.0\")`, and new `Handle[Image]` values coexist while blocks are upgraded.\n+\n+## Multi-Stage Orchestration (Comfy ⇄ Python)\n+\n+The primary mixing model is:\n+\n+```text\n+Graph A\n+  -> typed outputs / files / metadata\n+  -> ordinary Python transform or decision\n+  -> Graph B\n+```\n+\n+Arbitrary Python runs before, after, and between graph executions. It can inspect `RunResult`, read and write files, branch on metadata, update prompts, choose models, or decide which graph should run next.\n+\n+Arbitrary Python does not run inside an active Comfy graph unless it is compiled to an `ExternalPythonNode`. This boundary is deliberate: Comfy graph execution exchanges serialized node inputs and outputs, not arbitrary live Python objects.\n+\n+`VibeFlow` is the orchestration container for this model. It holds an ordered list of `Stage` entries, where each stage is either:\n+\n+- a `VibeWorkflow` to compile and execute through HiddenSwitch; or\n+- a `Callable[..., StageResult]` that runs ordinary Python and returns typed values, files, artifacts, or metadata for later stages.\n+\n+The executor passes typed media handles between stages. A Python stage can receive an image artifact from Graph A, write a transformed file, and pass that file handle into Graph B without pretending the transform was part of either Comfy graph.\n+\n+## ExternalPythonNode (Distinct from @block)\n+\n+`@block` is authoring-time sugar. The protocol at `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38` mutates a `VibeWorkflow` and returns `Handles`; it never executes during Comfy runtime.\n+\n+`ExternalPythonNode` is a runtime primitive. It emits a real Comfy custom node, or an external-process custom node, registered through `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`. Its inputs and outputs cross explicit serialization boundaries: typed file handles, tensor/media handles, strings, numbers, and other Comfy-compatible values only. No live Python objects cross that boundary.\n+\n+The names stay different on purpose. `@block` helps Python authors build a graph. `ExternalPythonNode` is the only sanctioned way for VibeComfy to run Python inside an active graph.\n+\n+| Surface | When it runs | Where it lives | Returns |\n+| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n+| `@block` (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) | Authoring time | Python builder code | `Handles` |\n+| `ExternalPythonNode` | Comfy runtime | Compiled to a Comfy custom node + `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` | Comfy-typed outputs (IMAGE/LATENT/STRING/…) |\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.
- Subagent design critique to fold into final document/review: guide users by making the desired structure the shortest path. Common intents include ready-template overrides, multi-stage graph execution, graph -> Python -> graph transforms, opaque subgraph reuse, and manual ready-template migration. Public concepts should be Template, typed Handle[T], RunResult/Artifact, Pipeline/ordinary async Python. First-class: ready Python builders, blocks, patches, typed requirements, multi-run orchestration, artifact passing. Escape hatches: raw add_node/connect, raw refs, opaque subgraphs, compile('api'/'graphbuilder'), raw API import/export, raw HiddenSwitch config. Add guardrail/lint ideas: untyped_raw_ref, stale_metadata, unbound_input_unused, artifact_vs_node_handle, ready_not_runtime_green, widget_index_escape, unknown_custom_node_commit, http_queue_only. Emphasize arbitrary Python belongs at orchestration boundaries; runtime Python inside a graph must be a custom/external node. Every high-level object should reveal lower layers for debugging: template.workflow, handle.node_id/slot, result.outputs, wf.compile('api'), wf.explain(), wf.doctor().

      "description": "Append three custom-nodes sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

      "id": "T5",
      "description": "Append three relationship/limits sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder` \u2014 keep `compile(\"graphbuilder\")` *optional* per `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md` (SD-001). Why GraphBuilder is **not** the authoring surface: ties authoring to an installed runtime, no typed handles beyond `.out(int)`, no provenance/metadata, no diff-friendly Python output. Borrow GraphBuilder's `id=` argument convention; use it as a parity-test target. (2) `## Relationship to ComfyScript` \u2014 `learn`, not `adopt` (SD-002). Future one-way import adapter only (ComfyScript script \u2192 `VibeWorkflow`). Reasoning: requires live `/object_info`, emits ComfyScript-flavored APIs, breaks on `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes (cite `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`). (3) `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` \u2014 HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Workarounds: (i) author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run via `run_embedded_sync`; (ii) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner that compiles a minimal graph terminating at a sink inferred from the handle's output type. **Prerequisite: `wf.run_until` is gated on the typed-`Handle` rollout AND on a populated schema snapshot (lands in P4). Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves. Do not promise inferred taps before typed handles land.** Cite `docs/runtime_surface.md` and `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`.",

Adding a new pack defaults to session reload or restart:\n+\n+```text\n+session.stop()\n+install or update nodepack\n+session.start()\n+```\n+\n+That default matches the existing lifecycle shape in `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py`: the session protocol has `start()` and `stop()` methods (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py:72`, `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py:84`), embedded sessions open a `Comfy()` context on start (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py:95`), embedded sessions close that context on stop (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py:169`), and server-session reconfiguration already uses `stop()` before `start()` when the runtime argv changes (`vibecomfy/runtime/session.py:255`).\n+\n+Embedded sessions reload by closing and reopening the `Comfy()` context. Server sessions restart `comfyui serve`. External server mode can report that a restart is required, but it should not terminate a server it does not own.\n+\n+An optional Python import-and-load path may exist later, but only behind a feature flag and only for proven-safe packs: no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU initialization, and no runtime monkey-patching that assumes process startup order. It is never the default path.\n+\n+## Custom Nodes — Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles\n+\n+SD-006 is settled: custom node dependencies are pinned by repository and immutable commit when specified; otherwise VibeComfy may resolve newest once and capture the result in the lockfile. The current lockfile already records pinned entries for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper` (`custom_nodes.lock:1`, `custom_nodes.lock:2`), matching the existing custom-node documentation (`docs/custom_nodes.md:3`).\n+\n+The lockfile fields should use precise names:\n+\n+- **`git_commit_sha`** - required primary pin for locked Git nodepacks. It is the immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repository and pins the whole repository tree at lock time.\n+- **`semantic_label`** - optional advisory label such as a tag, branch, or human name like `v1.4.0` or `main`. It resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time and is never enough to install by itself.\n+- **`source_sha256`** - optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time. Use it only for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources or expose many classes where class-level provenance matters. Skip it for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes.\n+\n+The distinction is load-bearing: `git_commit_sha` is a Git repository-object hash that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a content digest of one class file. They can coexist, but neither replaces the other.\n+\n+`custom_nodes.lock` remains the package-level manifest and is extended to record the new fields. A future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it after resolving `semantic_label` values, installing nodepacks, and optionally computing any per-class `source_sha256` values.\n+\n+## Custom Nodes — Authoring Surface\n+\n+The schema source for custom-node authoring is the combination of `/object_info` snapshot, `node_index.json`, and `custom_nodes.lock`. The authoring layer must work when a class is known only opaquely.\n+\n+The generic path is always available:\n+\n+```python\n+node = wf.node(class_type=\"SomeCustomNode\", **inputs)\n+```\n+\n+That path also covers UUID subgraph classes and opaque nodes. The existing opaque subgraph helper proves the pattern by preserving unknown class types and returning string-compatible handles from `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47`.\n+\n+Hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs such as Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, and ACE Step. They should improve ergonomics for common workflows, not become a prerequisite for using a custom node.\n+\n+Out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 runtime node classes. The local runtime surface records 1,202 node definitions from `/object_info` (`docs/runtime_surface.md:36`), so the default design must remain generic-first and schema-assisted rather than wrapper-complete.\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md

        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.
- Subagent design critique to fold into final document/review: guide users by making the desired structure the shortest path. Common intents include ready-template overrides, multi-stage graph execution, graph -> Python -> graph transforms, opaque subgraph reuse, and manual ready-template migration. Public concepts should be Template, typed Handle[T], RunResult/Artifact, Pipeline/ordinary async Python. First-class: ready Python builders, blocks, patches, typed requirements, multi-run orchestration, artifact passing. Escape hatches: raw add_node/connect, raw refs, opaque subgraphs, compile('api'/'graphbuilder'), raw API import/export, raw HiddenSwitch config. Add guardrail/lint ideas: untyped_raw_ref, stale_metadata, unbound_input_unused, artifact_vs_node_handle, ready_not_runtime_green, widget_index_escape, unknown_custom_node_commit, http_queue_only. Emphasize arbitrary Python belongs at orchestration boundaries; runtime Python inside a graph must be a custom/external node. Every high-level object should reveal lower layers for debugging: template.workflow, handle.node_id/slot, result.outputs, wf.compile('api'), wf.explain(), wf.doctor().

State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

    {
      "id": "T3",
      "description": "Append three sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md` covering the typed-handle / orchestration / runtime-Python triad. (1) `## Typed Handles (and Backward Compatibility)` \u2014 define `Handle` as carrying `(node_id, output_slot, output_type | None, name | None)`. State explicitly that `str(handle)` returns the existing `f\"{node_id}.{slot}\"` form so all current consumers keep working unchanged; cite `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47` and `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:12` (which wraps arbitrary values today, making the typed `Handle` additive). New code can opt into `.out(\"image\")` and `.type` accessors; recipes adopt incrementally \u2014 no flag day. (2) `## Multi-Stage Orchestration (Comfy \u21c4 Python)` \u2014 primary mixing model is `Graph A \u2192 typed outputs/files/metadata \u2192 ordinary Python transform/decision \u2192 Graph B`. State the rules: arbitrary Python runs *between* graph executions; arbitrary Python runs *inside* a graph only by compiling to an `ExternalPythonNode`. Define `VibeFlow` as holding an ordered list of `Stage` entries (each is either a `VibeWorkflow` to execute or a `Callable[..., StageResult]`); the executor passes typed media handles between stages. (3) `## ExternalPythonNode (Distinct from @block)` \u2014 explicit FLAG-005-resolving subsection. State that the authoring-time `@block` protocol (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) **mutates a `VibeWorkflow` and returns `Handles`** and **never executes during Comfy runtime**; that `ExternalPythonNode` is a **runtime** primitive emitting a real Comfy custom node (or external-process node) registered through `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` with explicit serialization boundaries on its inputs and outputs (typed file/tensor handles only \u2014 no Python objects across the boundary); that the two are deliberately named differently to prevent collision; that `@block` is authoring-only sugar and `ExternalPythonNode` is the only sanctioned way to run Python inside an active graph. Include this comparison table verbatim:\n\n| Surface | When it runs | Where it lives | Returns |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `@block` (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) | Authoring time | Python builder code | `Handles` |\n| `ExternalPythonNode` | Comfy runtime | Compiled to a Comfy custom node + `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` | Comfy-typed outputs (IMAGE/LATENT/STRING/\u2026) |",

Multi-Stage Orchestration frames Graph A -> typed outputs/files/metadata -> ordinary Python transform/decision -> Graph B as the primary mixing model, defines VibeFlow as ordered Stage entries, and states arbitrary Python runs between graph executions while in-graph Python requires ExternalPythonNode. ExternalPythonNode has its own H2, distinguishes runtime node execution from authoring-time @block, cites vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38, names NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS, describes serialization boundaries, and includes the required comparison table with Authoring time and Comfy runtime rows.       "id": "T4",
      "description": "Append three custom-nodes sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

      "id": "T5",
      "description": "Append three relationship/limits sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder` \u2014 keep `compile(\"graphbuilder\")` *optional* per `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md` (SD-001). Why GraphBuilder is **not** the authoring surface: ties authoring to an installed runtime, no typed handles beyond `.out(int)`, no provenance/metadata, no diff-friendly Python output. Borrow GraphBuilder's `id=` argument convention; use it as a parity-test target. (2) `## Relationship to ComfyScript` \u2014 `learn`, not `adopt` (SD-002). Future one-way import adapter only (ComfyScript script \u2192 `VibeWorkflow`). Reasoning: requires live `/object_info`, emits ComfyScript-flavored APIs, breaks on `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes (cite `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`). (3) `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` \u2014 HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Workarounds: (i) author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run via `run_embedded_sync`; (ii) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner that compiles a minimal graph terminating at a sink inferred from the handle's output type. **Prerequisite: `wf.run_until` is gated on the typed-`Handle` rollout AND on a populated schema snapshot (lands in P4). Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves. Do not promise inferred taps before typed handles land.** Cite `docs/runtime_surface.md` and `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`.",

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md\n@@\n Out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 runtime node classes. The local runtime surface records 1,202 node definitions from `/object_info` (`docs/runtime_surface.md:36`), so the default design must remain generic-first and schema-assisted rather than wrapper-complete.\n+\n+## Relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder\n+\n+GraphBuilder stays optional. That matches the existing spike decision to enable it as an optional backend (`docs/graphbuilder_spike.md:3`) and the current `VibeWorkflow.compile(\"graphbuilder\")` implementation path (`vibecomfy/workflow.py:184`).\n+\n+GraphBuilder is not the authoring surface:\n+\n+- it ties authoring to an installed HiddenSwitch runtime;\n+- it exposes only positional `.out(int)` outputs rather than typed `Handle[T]` names;\n+- it does not carry VibeComfy provenance, template metadata, or composition traces; and\n+- it does not produce diff-friendly VibeComfy Python builders.\n+\n+VibeComfy should borrow GraphBuilder's `id=` convention for explicit node IDs and use GraphBuilder as a parity-test target. The primary authoring path remains Python composition -> `VibeWorkflow` -> `compile(\"api\")`.\n+\n+## Relationship to ComfyScript\n+\n+ComfyScript is \"learn\", not \"adopt\". That is the current spike decision (`docs/comfyscript_spike.md:3`) and it remains the plan.\n+\n+The useful future shape is a one-way import adapter:\n+\n+```text\n+ComfyScript script -> VibeWorkflow\n+```\n+\n+It is not the core VibeComfy authoring API because the spike found three mismatches: the transpiler requires a live Comfy server and `/object_info` (`docs/comfyscript_spike.md:9`), successful output uses ComfyScript-flavored calls rather than `VibeWorkflow` edits (`docs/comfyscript_spike.md:19`), and common UI-only nodes such as `MarkdownNote` broke several conversions (`docs/comfyscript_spike.md:13`).\n+\n+Use ComfyScript as evidence for node naming, examples, and possible import/export ergonomics. Do not block template ingestion, scratchpad editing, or execution on ComfyScript.\n+\n+## Limits of Direct Node Execution\n+\n+HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs. The HTTP surface queues a prompt with `POST /prompt {\"prompt\": <api workflow dict>}` (`docs/runtime_surface.md:32`), and the embedded surface calls `comfy.queue_prompt_api(api_workflow)` (`docs/runtime_surface.md:50`). There is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API for executing one Comfy node in isolation.\n+\n+Supported workarounds:\n+\n+- Author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run it through the normal runtime path, such as `run_embedded_sync`.\n+- Later, provide `wf.run_until(handle)` as a debug runner that compiles a minimal graph ending at a sink inferred from the handle's output type.\n+\n+`wf.run_until(handle)` is gated on P4. It requires the typed-`Handle` rollout and a populated schema snapshot so the handle has an `output_type`. Until handles carry `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users manually attach `SaveImage`, `PreviewImage`, `SaveAudio`, or another appropriate sink.\n+\n+Do not promise inferred save/preview/audio taps before typed handles and schema metadata land. Runtime lifecycle remains graph-level: sessions start, run a compiled workflow, flush or reconfigure, and stop (`docs/runtime_lifecycle.md:9`, `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md:26`).\n*** End Patch\n"
}

Author the planned document sections.

        Project directory:
        /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/vibecomfy

        Output path (write all sections here):
        docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md

        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.
- Subagent design critique to fold into final document/review: guide users by making the desired structure the shortest path. Common intents include ready-template overrides, multi-stage graph execution, graph -> Python -> graph transforms, opaque subgraph reuse, and manual ready-template migration. Public concepts should be Template, typed Handle[T], RunResult/Artifact, Pipeline/ordinary async Python. First-class: ready Python builders, blocks, patches, typed requirements, multi-run orchestration, artifact passing. Escape hatches: raw add_node/connect, raw refs, opaque subgraphs, compile('api'/'graphbuilder'), raw API import/export, raw HiddenSwitch config. Add guardrail/lint ideas: untyped_raw_ref, stale_metadata, unbound_input_unused, artifact_vs_node_handle, ready_not_runtime_green, widget_index_escape, unknown_custom_node_commit, http_queue_only. Emphasize arbitrary Python belongs at orchestration boundaries; runtime Python inside a graph must be a custom/external node. Every high-level object should reveal lower layers for debugging: template.workflow, handle.node_id/slot, result.outputs, wf.compile('api'), wf.explain(), wf.doctor().

(2) `## Multi-Stage Orchestration (Comfy \u21c4 Python)` \u2014 primary mixing model is `Graph A \u2192 typed outputs/files/metadata \u2192 ordinary Python transform/decision \u2192 Graph B`. State the rules: arbitrary Python runs *between* graph executions; arbitrary Python runs *inside* a graph only by compiling to an `ExternalPythonNode`. Define `VibeFlow` as holding an ordered list of `Stage` entries (each is either a `VibeWorkflow` to execute or a `Callable[..., StageResult]`); the executor passes typed media handles between stages. (3) `## ExternalPythonNode (Distinct from @block)` \u2014 explicit FLAG-005-resolving subsection. State that the authoring-time `@block` protocol (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) **mutates a `VibeWorkflow` and returns `Handles`** and **never executes during Comfy runtime**; that `ExternalPythonNode` is a **runtime** primitive emitting a real Comfy custom node (or external-process node) registered through `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` with explicit serialization boundaries on its inputs and outputs (typed file/tensor handles only \u2014 no Python objects across the boundary); that the two are deliberately named differently to prevent collision; that `@block` is authoring-only sugar and `ExternalPythonNode` is the only sanctioned way to run Python inside an active graph. Include this comparison table verbatim:\n\n| Surface | When it runs | Where it lives | Returns |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `@block` (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) | Authoring time | Python builder code | `Handles` |\n| `ExternalPythonNode` | Comfy runtime | Compiled to a Comfy custom node + `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` | Comfy-typed outputs (IMAGE/LATENT/STRING/\u2026) |",

Multi-Stage Orchestration frames Graph A -> typed outputs/files/metadata -> ordinary Python transform/decision -> Graph B as the primary mixing model, defines VibeFlow as ordered Stage entries, and states arbitrary Python runs between graph executions while in-graph Python requires ExternalPythonNode. (1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

(3) `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` \u2014 HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves.       "description": "Append three sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md` covering the typed-handle / orchestration / runtime-Python triad. (1) `## Typed Handles (and Backward Compatibility)` \u2014 define `Handle` as carrying `(node_id, output_slot, output_type | None, name | None)`. State explicitly that `str(handle)` returns the existing `f\"{node_id}.{slot}\"` form so all current consumers keep working unchanged; cite `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47` and `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:12` (which wraps arbitrary values today, making the typed `Handle` additive). New code can opt into `.out(\"image\")` and `.type` accessors; recipes adopt incrementally \u2014 no flag day. (2) `## Multi-Stage Orchestration (Comfy \u21c4 Python)` \u2014 primary mixing model is `Graph A \u2192 typed outputs/files/metadata \u2192 ordinary Python transform/decision \u2192 Graph B`. State the rules: arbitrary Python runs *between* graph executions; arbitrary Python runs *inside* a graph only by compiling to an `ExternalPythonNode`. Define `VibeFlow` as holding an ordered list of `Stage` entries (each is either a `VibeWorkflow` to execute or a `Callable[..., StageResult]`); the executor passes typed media handles between stages. (3) `## ExternalPythonNode (Distinct from @block)` \u2014 explicit FLAG-005-resolving subsection. State that the authoring-time `@block` protocol (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) **mutates a `VibeWorkflow` and returns `Handles`** and **never executes during Comfy runtime**; that `ExternalPythonNode` is a **runtime** primitive emitting a real Comfy custom node (or external-process node) registered through `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` with explicit serialization boundaries on its inputs and outputs (typed file/tensor handles only \u2014 no Python objects across the boundary); that the two are deliberately named differently to prevent collision; that `@block` is authoring-only sugar and `ExternalPythonNode` is the only sanctioned way to run Python inside an active graph. Include this comparison table verbatim:\n\n| Surface | When it runs | Where it lives | Returns |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `@block` (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) | Authoring time | Python builder code | `Handles` |\n| `ExternalPythonNode` | Comfy runtime | Compiled to a Comfy custom node + `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` | Comfy-typed outputs (IMAGE/LATENT/STRING/\u2026) |",

      "description": "Append three custom-nodes sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",
