(1) `## Relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder` \u2014 keep `compile(\"graphbuilder\")` *optional* per `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md` (SD-001). Why GraphBuilder is **not** the authoring surface: ties authoring to an installed runtime, no typed handles beyond `.out(int)`, no provenance/metadata, no diff-friendly Python output. Borrow GraphBuilder's `id=` argument convention; use it as a parity-test target. (2) `## Relationship to ComfyScript` \u2014 `learn`, not `adopt` (SD-002). Future one-way import adapter only (ComfyScript script \u2192 `VibeWorkflow`). Reasoning: requires live `/object_info`, emits ComfyScript-flavored APIs, breaks on `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes (cite `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`). (3) `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` \u2014 HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Workarounds: (i) author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run via `run_embedded_sync`; (ii) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner that compiles a minimal graph terminating at a sink inferred from the handle's output type. **Prerequisite: `wf.run_until` is gated on the typed-`Handle` rollout AND on a populated schema snapshot (lands in P4). Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves. Do not promise inferred taps before typed handles land.** Cite `docs/runtime_surface.md` and `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`.",

(2) `## Open Questions` \u2014 surface the four planner questions: VibeFlow failure-mode semantics (retry/cancel of remaining stages on Python-transform error vs leave to caller); whether `Handle` should carry source `block_id`/recipe context for debugging; whether raw API-dict pass-through should strip MarkdownNote-style UI nodes or preserve verbatim and let validation flag; ExternalPythonNode codegen first-cut \u2014 external subprocess boundary vs in-process custom-node module.   "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md\n@@\n Do not promise inferred save/preview/audio taps before typed handles and schema metadata land. Runtime lifecycle remains graph-level: sessions start, run a compiled workflow, flush or reconfigure, and stop (`docs/runtime_lifecycle.md:9`, `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md:26`).\n+\n+## Ready Template Migration\n+\n+The repository already shows both sides of the migration. There are 42 ready-template `.py` files, 41 of them currently embed `API_WORKFLOW` dictionaries, and one is already a native VibeComfy Python builder. `ready_templates/image/z_image.py:6` is representative of the API-dict precedent; `ready_templates/image/flux2_klein_4b_t2i.py:81` is the native builder precedent and the migration model.\n+\n+Migration policy:\n+\n+- Leave runtime-green API-dict templates untouched until they need editing.\n+- When a template is being tweaked, re-author it through the composition layer using the Flux 4B builder shape.\n+- Keep round-trippability: every authored template should `compile(\"api\")` to a graph that matches its prior runtime-green API JSON within an allowed-difference set.\n+\n+Allowed differences should stay narrow: node IDs may move when the authored builder intentionally chooses clearer IDs, and UI-only `MarkdownNote` nodes may be stripped when they have no runtime effect. Behavioral graph nodes, model names, prompts, dimensions, sampler settings, and output sinks should remain explainably equivalent.\n+\n+Manual/iterative migration is the policy. There is no big-bang codemod, and this plan does not promise a codemod CLI. The right migration moment is when a ready template is being touched for a product reason: a model update, a parameter override, a new block, a broken runtime dependency, or a documentation improvement.\n+\n+## Reconciliation with docs/authoring.md\n+\n+`docs/authoring.md:99` says ready templates \"should be hand-curated Python builders\" and that retiring the materializer is intentional. This plan softens, not supersedes, that rule: hand-curated Python builders remain the destination shape, and the Flux 4B builder is the proof point, but the plan accepts the current 41-of-42 API-dict reality and migrates manually when a template is edited.\n+\n+A one-line pointer added to `docs/authoring.md` in this same delivery links readers back to this plan. The core authoring rules in `docs/authoring.md` remain authoritative: blocks mutate workflows and return handles, patches decorate or transform workflows, edge primitives support topology changes, and `finalize_metadata()` refreshes discoverable inputs, outputs, and requirements.\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.
- Subagent design critique to fold into final document/review: guide users by making the desired structure the shortest path. Common intents include ready-template overrides, multi-stage graph execution, graph -> Python -> graph transforms, opaque subgraph reuse, and manual ready-template migration. Public concepts should be Template, typed Handle[T], RunResult/Artifact, Pipeline/ordinary async Python. First-class: ready Python builders, blocks, patches, typed requirements, multi-run orchestration, artifact passing. Escape hatches: raw add_node/connect, raw refs, opaque subgraphs, compile('api'/'graphbuilder'), raw API import/export, raw HiddenSwitch config. Add guardrail/lint ideas: untyped_raw_ref, stale_metadata, unbound_input_unused, artifact_vs_node_handle, ready_not_runtime_green, widget_index_escape, unknown_custom_node_commit, http_queue_only. Emphasize arbitrary Python belongs at orchestration boundaries; runtime Python inside a graph must be a custom/external node. Every high-level object should reveal lower layers for debugging: template.workflow, handle.node_id/slot, result.outputs, wf.compile('api'), wf.explain(), wf.doctor().

(2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

The composition layer should extend that pattern upward instead of replacing it.\n+\n+Add `workflow.metadata[\"composition_trace\"]` as an ordered list of block, recipe, template, patch, and stage calls. Each entry should record the public callable name, stable call ID when available, selected kwargs, and output handles. This gives `wf.explain()` and analysis commands a readable path from Python authoring calls to graph nodes.\n+\n+Populate `workflow.source.provenance` on the existing source container (`vibecomfy/workflow.py:10`) with structured source data such as:\n+\n+```python\n+{\n+    \"authored\": True,\n+    \"recipe\": \"image.t2i\",\n+    \"source_template\": \"image/z_image\",\n+    \"builder_path\": \"<abs path>\",\n+}\n+```\n+\n+Keep `metadata.subgraph_class_type` for opaque UUID subgraphs. The authoring guide already documents that opaque subgraphs preserve UUID class types and set that metadata field (`docs/authoring.md:84`).\n+\n+For multi-stage flows, write `flow_trace` to `out/runs/<run-id>/metadata.json`. It should record each stage type, stage inputs, stage outputs, artifact paths, selected runtime backend, and node-pack lock SHAs, including both `git_commit_sha` and any per-class `source_sha256`.\n+\n+## Debugging\n+\n+Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. The initial affordances should be:\n+\n+- `wf.show()` and `vibecomfy analyze info` print the composition trace alongside the node graph.\n+- `wf.run_until(handle)` compiles a minimal graph that terminates at a sink inferred from the handle type.\n+- `wf.tap(\"save_image\", from_handle)` inserts a preview/save tap without rewriting downstream wiring.\n+\n+`wf.run_until(handle)` is available only after typed handles and schema-backed output types land in P4. Until then, authors should manually attach the relevant `SaveImage`, `PreviewImage`, `SaveAudio`, or other sink node.\n+\n+Reuse the old analysis backlog for before/after reasoning. The port rationale explicitly calls out `analyze`, `trace`, and `diff` as workflow-understanding commands to carry forward (`docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md:25`).\n+\n+## Layered Debug Affordances\n+\n+Every high-level object should reveal its lower layer. The composition API should make the desired path short without hiding the graph that will run.\n+\n+- `template.workflow` exposes the underlying `VibeWorkflow`.\n+- `handle.node_id` and `handle.slot` expose the graph output identity.\n+- `result.outputs` exposes runtime outputs and named artifacts.\n+- `wf.compile(\"api\")` exposes the exact Comfy API dictionary.\n+- `wf.explain()` shows composition trace, node provenance, and requirements.\n+- `wf.doctor()` checks missing models, custom nodes, stale metadata, and schema mismatches.\n+\n+## Lint / Guardrail Ideas\n+\n+These are future static-analysis ideas, not P1 deliverables:\n+\n+- `untyped_raw_ref` - a string ref is used where a typed `Handle[T]` is available.\n+- `stale_metadata` - nodes changed after the last `finalize_metadata()` or trace update.\n+- `unbound_input_unused` - a declared prompt/seed/model override is never bound to a node.\n+- `artifact_vs_node_handle` - a Python artifact path is passed where a Comfy node handle is required, or the reverse.\n+- `ready_not_runtime_green` - a ready template migration lacks runtime parity evidence.\n+- `widget_index_escape` - code uses `widget_N` for a node whose schema has named inputs.\n+- `unknown_custom_node_commit` - a custom nodepack is used without a locked `git_commit_sha`.\n+- `http_queue_only` - a test or debug path relies only on HTTP queue submission when completed outputs are required.\n+\n+## Escape Hatches\n+\n+Escape hatches stay public, but each one loses some provenance unless the caller fills it back in:\n+\n+- Raw JSON load via `convert_to_vibe_format` (`vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py:19`). This preserves graph structure but may not know the original recipe, block call stack, or source builder path.\n+- Raw node construction via `wf.add_node(class_type, **inputs)`. This preserves the node in `VibeWorkflow` but skips block-level `block`, `block_id`, and `widget_kwargs` metadata unless the caller sets it.\n+- Raw API-dict pass-through by assembling a dict and calling `Comfy.queue_prompt_api` directly. This bypasses `VibeWorkflow`, so VibeComfy loses composition trace, typed handles, patch provenance, requirements inference, and schema-backed validation unless the caller separately records them.\n*** End Patch\n"
}
```

        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.
- Subagent design critique to fold into final document/review: guide users by making the desired structure the shortest path. Common intents include ready-template overrides, multi-stage graph execution, graph -> Python -> graph transforms, opaque subgraph reuse, and manual ready-template migration. Public concepts should be Template, typed Handle[T], RunResult/Artifact, Pipeline/ordinary async Python. First-class: ready Python builders, blocks, patches, typed requirements, multi-run orchestration, artifact passing. Escape hatches: raw add_node/connect, raw refs, opaque subgraphs, compile('api'/'graphbuilder'), raw API import/export, raw HiddenSwitch config. Add guardrail/lint ideas: untyped_raw_ref, stale_metadata, unbound_input_unused, artifact_vs_node_handle, ready_not_runtime_green, widget_index_escape, unknown_custom_node_commit, http_queue_only. Emphasize arbitrary Python belongs at orchestration boundaries; runtime Python inside a graph must be a custom/external node. Every high-level object should reveal lower layers for debugging: template.workflow, handle.node_id/slot, result.outputs, wf.compile('api'), wf.explain(), wf.doctor().

    "id": "T4",
    "description": "Append three custom-nodes sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

Pinning names git_commit_sha, semantic_label, and source_sha256 exactly, cites custom_nodes.lock:1-2 and docs/custom_nodes.md:3, and explicitly distinguishes git_commit_sha as a Git repository-object hash from source_sha256 as a per-class content digest.     "id": "T5",
    "description": "Append three relationship/limits sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder` \u2014 keep `compile(\"graphbuilder\")` *optional* per `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md` (SD-001). Why GraphBuilder is **not** the authoring surface: ties authoring to an installed runtime, no typed handles beyond `.out(int)`, no provenance/metadata, no diff-friendly Python output. Borrow GraphBuilder's `id=` argument convention; use it as a parity-test target. (2) `## Relationship to ComfyScript` \u2014 `learn`, not `adopt` (SD-002). Future one-way import adapter only (ComfyScript script \u2192 `VibeWorkflow`). Reasoning: requires live `/object_info`, emits ComfyScript-flavored APIs, breaks on `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes (cite `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`). (3) `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` \u2014 HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Workarounds: (i) author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run via `run_embedded_sync`; (ii) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner that compiles a minimal graph terminating at a sink inferred from the handle's output type. **Prerequisite: `wf.run_until` is gated on the typed-`Handle` rollout AND on a populated schema snapshot (lands in P4). Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves. Do not promise inferred taps before typed handles land.** Cite `docs/runtime_surface.md` and `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`.",

(1) `## Typed Handles (and Backward Compatibility)` \u2014 define `Handle` as carrying `(node_id, output_slot, output_type | None, name | None)`. State explicitly that `str(handle)` returns the existing `f\"{node_id}.{slot}\"` form so all current consumers keep working unchanged; cite `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47` and `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:12` (which wraps arbitrary values today, making the typed `Handle` additive). (2) `## Multi-Stage Orchestration (Comfy \u21c4 Python)` \u2014 primary mixing model is `Graph A \u2192 typed outputs/files/metadata \u2192 ordinary Python transform/decision \u2192 Graph B`. State the rules: arbitrary Python runs *between* graph executions; arbitrary Python runs *inside* a graph only by compiling to an `ExternalPythonNode`. Define `VibeFlow` as holding an ordered list of `Stage` entries (each is either a `VibeWorkflow` to execute or a `Callable[..., StageResult]`); the executor passes typed media handles between stages. (3) `## ExternalPythonNode (Distinct from @block)` \u2014 explicit FLAG-005-resolving subsection. State that the authoring-time `@block` protocol (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) **mutates a `VibeWorkflow` and returns `Handles`** and **never executes during Comfy runtime**; that `ExternalPythonNode` is a **runtime** primitive emitting a real Comfy custom node (or external-process node) registered through `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` with explicit serialization boundaries on its inputs and outputs (typed file/tensor handles only \u2014 no Python objects across the boundary); that the two are deliberately named differently to prevent collision; that `@block` is authoring-only sugar and `ExternalPythonNode` is the only sanctioned way to run Python inside an active graph. Include this comparison table verbatim:\n\n| Surface | When it runs | Where it lives | Returns |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `@block` (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) | Authoring time | Python builder code | `Handles` |\n| `ExternalPythonNode` | Comfy runtime | Compiled to a Comfy custom node + `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` | Comfy-typed outputs (IMAGE/LATENT/STRING/\u2026) |",

      "description": "Append three custom-nodes sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

Why GraphBuilder is **not** the authoring surface: ties authoring to an installed runtime, no typed handles beyond `.out(int)`, no provenance/metadata, no diff-friendly Python output. Future one-way import adapter only (ComfyScript script \u2192 `VibeWorkflow`). Reasoning: requires live `/object_info`, emits ComfyScript-flavored APIs, breaks on `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes (cite `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`). (3) `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` \u2014 HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Workarounds: (i) author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run via `run_embedded_sync`; (ii) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner that compiles a minimal graph terminating at a sink inferred from the handle's output type. **Prerequisite: `wf.run_until` is gated on the typed-`Handle` rollout AND on a populated schema snapshot (lands in P4). Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves. Reuse `analyze trace`/`analyze diff` (cite `docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md:25`) for before/after diffs. (3) `## Layered Debug Affordances` (per gate warning) \u2014 capture the principle that every high-level object reveals its lower layer: `template.workflow`, `handle.node_id`/`handle.slot`, `result.outputs`, `wf.compile(\"api\")`, `wf.explain()`, `wf.doctor()`. {
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md\n@@\n - Raw JSON load via `convert_to_vibe_format` (`vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py:19`). This preserves graph structure but may not know the original recipe, block call stack, or source builder path.\n - Raw node construction via `wf.add_node(class_type, **inputs)`. This preserves the node in `VibeWorkflow` but skips block-level `block`, `block_id`, and `widget_kwargs` metadata unless the caller sets it.\n - Raw API-dict pass-through by assembling a dict and calling `Comfy.queue_prompt_api` directly. This bypasses `VibeWorkflow`, so VibeComfy loses composition trace, typed handles, patch provenance, requirements inference, and schema-backed validation unless the caller separately records them.\n+\n+## Rollout\n+\n+Each phase is gated on the previous phase. Do not move `wf.run_until(handle)` earlier than P4.\n+\n+- **P1** - Add typed `Handle` API on top of the current IR with string-coerce backward compatibility; add `wf.node(...)` as the typed wrapper over node creation; add parity tests against `compile(\"graphbuilder\")`.\n+- **P2** - Expand block library coverage for Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, and ACE Step; replace positional `widget_N` keys with named widgets where schema is known.\n+- **P3** - Add `VibeFlow` multi-stage orchestration for Comfy <-> Python with typed media handles; add `ExternalPythonNode` codegen for rare cases that need Python at graph-execution time; add custom-node session-reload helpers; add `vibecomfy nodes lock` writing `git_commit_sha`, optional `semantic_label`, and optional `source_sha256` to `custom_nodes.lock`.\n+- **P4** - Add schema-backed validation against a `/object_info` snapshot in CI, following the schema-validation direction in `docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md:9`; populate typed-handle `output_type`; ungate the `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner.\n+- **P5** - Migrate ready templates manually and iteratively, starting from highest-traffic templates and using the Flux 4B builder as the canonical shape; add the optional ComfyScript import adapter and any further public docs.\n+\n+`wf.run_until(handle)` lands in P4, not P1. P1 can create typed handles and preserve string compatibility, but it cannot infer safe save/preview/audio sinks until P4 provides schema-backed `output_type`.\n+\n+## Open Questions\n+\n+- What should `VibeFlow` failure-mode semantics be: retry/cancel remaining stages on Python-transform error, or leave retry/cancel policy to the caller?\n+- Should `Handle` carry source `block_id` or recipe context directly for debugging, or should that stay in the composition trace only?\n+- Should raw API-dict pass-through strip `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes, or preserve them verbatim and let validation flag runtime irrelevance?\n+- For the first `ExternalPythonNode` codegen path, should the boundary be an external subprocess or an in-process custom-node module?\n+\n+## Assumptions\n+\n+- `VibeWorkflow` remains the canonical IR.\n+- GraphBuilder remains an optional backend.\n+- ComfyScript remains import-only/reference material, not the authoring API.\n+- Direct single-node execution is out of scope; debug runs are gated on P4.\n+- Schema-backed validation uses a `/object_info` snapshot.\n+- Ready-template migration is manual and iterative, with the Flux 4B builder precedent.\n+- Custom-node pinning uses `(repo_url, git_commit_sha)` with optional `semantic_label` and `source_sha256`.\n+- Adding new nodepacks defaults to session reload or restart.\n+- Multi-stage orchestration is the primary mixing model for Comfy and Python.\n+- `ExternalPythonNode` is the path for Python that must run inside a Comfy graph.\n+- `@block` is authoring-time only; `ExternalPythonNode` is runtime.\n+- Typed `Handle` is additive and keeps `str()` coercion.\n+- The deliverable is two markdown edits: this plan plus the pointer in `docs/authoring.md`.\n+- The final document should stay within roughly 300-550 lines.\n+- Custom-node coverage starts generic-first with `wf.node(...)`, plus hand-curated blocks for high-traffic packs.\n*** End Patch\n"
}

        Idea:
Design a concise architecture plan for VibeComfy's pure-Python composable workflow authoring layer. Communicate goals and constraints succinctly. The user wants to tweak, refine, update, and combine workflows in Python while still executing through HiddenSwitch/Comfy. Address relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder and ComfyScript, limits of direct node execution, ready template migration, custom nodes, provenance, debugging, and rollout.

User notes and answers:
- User requirement: architecture must explicitly address adding custom nodes on the fly and mixing Comfy execution with ordinary non-Comfy Python code. Clarify supported modes: pre-run custom node install/discovery/sync is in scope; hot-loading into an already-running HiddenSwitch executor may require session restart/reload. Non-Comfy Python should be allowed before/after Comfy graph runs and between graph stages, using typed media/file handles; direct execution inside a Comfy graph node requires wrapping as a custom node or a VibeComfy external-python block with clear serialization boundaries.
- Additional user requirements: (1) custom node dependencies should support explicit per-nodepack pinning, preferably immutable commit SHA pins plus optional semantic labels; the plan should discuss hash types and lockfile/manifests at custom-node package level, and possibly node-class/source provenance level when nodepacks expose many classes. (2) Multi-stage orchestration should be first-class: execute graph A, collect typed outputs/files/metadata, run ordinary Python transforms or decisions, then pass results into graph B. This should be framed as the best default for mixing non-Comfy Python with Comfy, rather than trying to inject arbitrary Python into an active graph.
- Settled user decisions for the document: (1) A VibeComfy flow is Python orchestration that can contain multiple Comfy graph executions at various stages; each graph should ideally be expressible as code. (2) First-class typed values/handles are required. (3) Arbitrary Python runs before/after/between graph executions, not inside an active Comfy graph unless wrapped. (4) Custom nodes are pinned by repo/commit when specified, otherwise may default to newest with lockfile capture. (5) Adding custom nodes requires session reboot/reload by default, with optional Python import/loading only when safe and proven. (6) Debugging helpers depend on typed handles. (7) Provide default workflow/raw JSON/raw node escape hatches. (8) Ready templates migrate manually/iteratively like the Flux example, not big-bang. (9) Structured provenance is desired. (10) Graph output -> Python transform -> next graph is the primary multi-stage composition model.
- Subagent design critique to fold into final document/review: guide users by making the desired structure the shortest path. Common intents include ready-template overrides, multi-stage graph execution, graph -> Python -> graph transforms, opaque subgraph reuse, and manual ready-template migration. Public concepts should be Template, typed Handle[T], RunResult/Artifact, Pipeline/ordinary async Python. First-class: ready Python builders, blocks, patches, typed requirements, multi-run orchestration, artifact passing. Escape hatches: raw add_node/connect, raw refs, opaque subgraphs, compile('api'/'graphbuilder'), raw API import/export, raw HiddenSwitch config. Add guardrail/lint ideas: untyped_raw_ref, stale_metadata, unbound_input_unused, artifact_vs_node_handle, ready_not_runtime_green, widget_index_escape, unknown_custom_node_commit, http_queue_only. Emphasize arbitrary Python belongs at orchestration boundaries; runtime Python inside a graph must be a custom/external node. Every high-level object should reveal lower layers for debugging: template.workflow, handle.node_id/slot, result.outputs, wf.compile('api'), wf.explain(), wf.doctor().

Migration policy (SD-008): (a) leave runtime-green API-dict templates untouched until they need editing; (b) when a template is being tweaked, re-author it through the composition layer using the Flux 4B builder shape; (c) keep round-trippability \u2014 every authored template must `compile(\"api\")` to a graph that matches its prior runtime-green API JSON within an allowed-difference set (node IDs, MarkdownNote stripping). State explicitly: **manual/iterative migration is the policy; no big-bang codemod, no codemod CLI promised in this plan.** (2) `## Reconciliation with docs/authoring.md` \u2014 short paragraph: `docs/authoring.md:99` says ready templates 'should be hand-curated Python builders' and that retiring the materializer is intentional.       "id": "T3",
      "description": "Append three sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md` covering the typed-handle / orchestration / runtime-Python triad. (1) `## Typed Handles (and Backward Compatibility)` \u2014 define `Handle` as carrying `(node_id, output_slot, output_type | None, name | None)`. State explicitly that `str(handle)` returns the existing `f\"{node_id}.{slot}\"` form so all current consumers keep working unchanged; cite `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py:47` and `vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:12` (which wraps arbitrary values today, making the typed `Handle` additive). New code can opt into `.out(\"image\")` and `.type` accessors; recipes adopt incrementally \u2014 no flag day. (2) `## Multi-Stage Orchestration (Comfy \u21c4 Python)` \u2014 primary mixing model is `Graph A \u2192 typed outputs/files/metadata \u2192 ordinary Python transform/decision \u2192 Graph B`. State the rules: arbitrary Python runs *between* graph executions; arbitrary Python runs *inside* a graph only by compiling to an `ExternalPythonNode`. Define `VibeFlow` as holding an ordered list of `Stage` entries (each is either a `VibeWorkflow` to execute or a `Callable[..., StageResult]`); the executor passes typed media handles between stages. (3) `## ExternalPythonNode (Distinct from @block)` \u2014 explicit FLAG-005-resolving subsection. State that the authoring-time `@block` protocol (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) **mutates a `VibeWorkflow` and returns `Handles`** and **never executes during Comfy runtime**; that `ExternalPythonNode` is a **runtime** primitive emitting a real Comfy custom node (or external-process node) registered through `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` with explicit serialization boundaries on its inputs and outputs (typed file/tensor handles only \u2014 no Python objects across the boundary); that the two are deliberately named differently to prevent collision; that `@block` is authoring-only sugar and `ExternalPythonNode` is the only sanctioned way to run Python inside an active graph. Include this comparison table verbatim:\n\n| Surface | When it runs | Where it lives | Returns |\n| --- | --- | --- | --- |\n| `@block` (`vibecomfy/blocks/__init__.py:38`) | Authoring time | Python builder code | `Handles` |\n| `ExternalPythonNode` | Comfy runtime | Compiled to a Comfy custom node + `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS` | Comfy-typed outputs (IMAGE/LATENT/STRING/\u2026) |",

Multi-Stage Orchestration frames Graph A -> typed outputs/files/metadata -> ordinary Python transform/decision -> Graph B as the primary mixing model, defines VibeFlow as ordered Stage entries, and states arbitrary Python runs between graph executions while in-graph Python requires ExternalPythonNode.       "id": "T4",
      "description": "Append three custom-nodes sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Adding On the Fly` \u2014 supported modes: pre-run install/discovery/sync via extending `vibecomfy nodes install-plan` and `vibecomfy doctor` is in scope; hot-load into a running HiddenSwitch executor is NOT default \u2014 adding a new pack defaults to **session reload/restart** using existing `vibecomfy/runtime/session.py` lifecycle (`stop()` \u2192 install \u2192 `start()`); embedded sessions reload by closing/reopening the `Comfy()` context, server sessions restart `comfyui serve`; optional Python import-and-load path allowed only for proven-safe packs (no global side effects beyond `NODE_CLASS_MAPPINGS`, no module-level GPU init), behind a feature flag, never default. (2) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Pinning, Hashes, and Lockfiles` \u2014 name the hash types concretely (resolves issue_hints / FLAG-004): **`git_commit_sha`** required immutable Git commit SHA-1 of the nodepack repo, primary pin, already recorded in `custom_nodes.lock` for `ComfyUI-KJNodes` and `ComfyUI-WanVideoWrapper`; **`semantic_label`** optional advisory (tag/branch/human label like `v1.4.0` or `main`) that resolves to a `git_commit_sha` at lock time, never used at install time on its own; **`source_sha256`** optional per-node-class SHA-256 hex digest of an individual node-class source file's bytes at lock time, used **only** for packs that vendor classes from multiple upstream sources, skipped for typical packs whose `git_commit_sha` already pins all classes. State the distinction explicitly: `git_commit_sha` is a Git **repository-object hash** that pins the whole tree; `source_sha256` is a **content digest** of a single class file; the two coexist; neither replaces the other. Lockfile `custom_nodes.lock` (already exists) is extended to record the new fields; a future `vibecomfy nodes lock` command rewrites it. Reference SD-006; do not relitigate pinning strategy. Cite `custom_nodes.lock` and `docs/custom_nodes.md`. (3) `## Custom Nodes \u2014 Authoring Surface` \u2014 schema source: `/object_info` snapshot + `node_index.json` + `custom_nodes.lock`; authoring layer must work with **opaque** classes (e.g. UUID subgraph nodes per `vibecomfy/blocks/subgraph.py`) via the generic `wf.node(class_type=...)` path; hand-written blocks remain optional sugar for high-traffic packs (Wan, LTX, KJNodes, GGUF, ACE Step); out of scope: auto-generating typed Python wrappers for all 1,202 node classes.",

Pinning names git_commit_sha, semantic_label, and source_sha256 exactly, cites custom_nodes.lock:1-2 and docs/custom_nodes.md:3, and explicitly distinguishes git_commit_sha as a Git repository-object hash from source_sha256 as a per-class content digest.       "id": "T5",
      "description": "Append three relationship/limits sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Relationship to HiddenSwitch GraphBuilder` \u2014 keep `compile(\"graphbuilder\")` *optional* per `docs/graphbuilder_spike.md` (SD-001). Why GraphBuilder is **not** the authoring surface: ties authoring to an installed runtime, no typed handles beyond `.out(int)`, no provenance/metadata, no diff-friendly Python output. Borrow GraphBuilder's `id=` argument convention; use it as a parity-test target. (2) `## Relationship to ComfyScript` \u2014 `learn`, not `adopt` (SD-002). Future one-way import adapter only (ComfyScript script \u2192 `VibeWorkflow`). Reasoning: requires live `/object_info`, emits ComfyScript-flavored APIs, breaks on `MarkdownNote`-style UI nodes (cite `docs/comfyscript_spike.md`). (3) `## Limits of Direct Node Execution` \u2014 HiddenSwitch executes whole graphs; there is no `run_node(class_type, **inputs)` API. Workarounds: (i) author a one-node-plus-sink subgraph and run via `run_embedded_sync`; (ii) `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner that compiles a minimal graph terminating at a sink inferred from the handle's output type. **Prerequisite: `wf.run_until` is gated on the typed-`Handle` rollout AND on a populated schema snapshot (lands in P4). Until handles carry an `output_type`, the debug runner is unavailable; users fall back to manually attaching a `SaveImage`/`PreviewImage`/`SaveAudio` node themselves. Do not promise inferred taps before typed handles land.** Cite `docs/runtime_surface.md` and `docs/runtime_lifecycle.md`.",

      "id": "T8",
      "description": "Append the closing sections to `docs/python_composition_dsl_plan.md`. (1) `## Rollout` \u2014 five phases, each gated on the previous: **P1** typed `Handle` API on top of current IR with string-coerce backward compat; `wf.node(...)` typed wrapper; parity tests against `compile(\"graphbuilder\")`. **P2** expand block library coverage for Wan/LTX/KJNodes/GGUF/ACE Step; replace positional `widget_N` keys with named widgets where schema is known. **P3** `VibeFlow` multi-stage orchestration (Comfy \u21c4 Python with typed media handles); `ExternalPythonNode` codegen for the rare cases that need Python at graph-execution time; custom-node session-reload helpers; `vibecomfy nodes lock` writing `git_commit_sha` + optional `semantic_label` + optional `source_sha256` to `custom_nodes.lock`. **P4** schema-backed validation against `/object_info` snapshot in CI (cite `docs/old_vibecomfy_port_rationale.md:9`); typed-handle `output_type` populated; `wf.run_until(handle)` debug runner ungated. **P5** manual/iterative ready-template migration starting from highest-traffic templates, using the Flux 4B builder as the canonical shape; optional ComfyScript-import adapter and any further public docs. Be explicit that `wf.run_until` lands in P4, not P1. (2) `## Open Questions` \u2014 surface the four planner questions: VibeFlow failure-mode semantics (retry/cancel of remaining stages on Python-transform error vs leave to caller); whether `Handle` should carry source `block_id`/recipe context for debugging; whether raw API-dict pass-through should strip MarkdownNote-style UI nodes or preserve verbatim and let validation flag; ExternalPythonNode codegen first-cut \u2014 external subprocess boundary vs in-process custom-node module. (3) `## Assumptions` \u2014 list the planner assumptions covering: VibeWorkflow as canonical IR; GraphBuilder optional backend; ComfyScript import-only; direct single-node execution out of scope and debug runs gated on P4; schema-backed validation via `/object_info` snapshot; manual/iterative ready-template migration with Flux 4B precedent; pinning by `(repo_url, git_commit_sha)` with optional `semantic_label` and `source_sha256`; default session reload for new packs; multi-stage as primary mixing model with `ExternalPythonNode` for in-graph Python; `@block` authoring-time only vs `ExternalPythonNode` runtime; typed `Handle` additive with `str()` coercion; deliverable is two markdown edits; doc length ~300\u2013550 lines; custom-node coverage starts with generic `wf.node(...)` plus hand-curated blocks for high-traffic packs.",
